WHY: RP-11/QF-11 — "every failure speaks the same machine-readable language from API to UI". Three ad-hoc shapes and two code-cases existed (`ApiError.code` lowercase, answer `reason_code` UPPER, worker literals lowercase-or-UPPER) with **no** `packages/core/errors.py` in the tree even though `packages/answer/models.py` and `packages/graph/extractor.py` both *cite* `core.errors.ReasonCode` as the canonical registry and `apps/web` already mirrored a `core.errors` union — i.e. the contract was referenced from four places and defined in zero (a previous agent's Q0 file was wiped from the tree by the documented "mystery waves"; durable copy at `/tmp/opencode/q0_files/errors.py` informed the design but the file here is a **re-derivation** against the current tree, with the required-vs-optional dependency matrix, the status enums and the disclosure codes that the ticket demands and that copy lacks). DECISIONS.md row: One-framework/adapter-boundary — stdlib `enum`+`dataclasses` only, no vendor error framework (RFC-7807 would have frozen the existing `{"detail":{"error","message"}}` contract and broken `apps/web`).

REUSED: `/tmp/opencode/q0_files/{errors.py,test_error_taxonomy_contract.py}` — the durable copy of the earlier TASK-Q0 attempt (already attributed as custom in `handoffs/CODE1-Q0-HANDOFF.md`), read as the reference for the registry/`ErrorSpec.wire`/`LEGACY_ALIASES` shape and the 18 legacy-pinned API wire values. No third-party code, no new dependency, no REUSE_MAP/NOTICES delta.

BUILT: the taxonomy module itself (~40 registry entries, the two status enums, the status↔reason maps and the dependency matrix) because nothing in-tree or upstream fits: the repo needs a *frozen-wire* registry with per-mode dependency criticality, and pulling in an error-handling framework would move the wire contract rather than collapse it. `DEPENDENCY_MATRIX` is classified from what the code actually touches (`apps/api/app/adapters/readiness.py` probe set, `ingest_pipeline.py`, `packages/retrieval`, `packages/answer`), with two documented judgement calls: (a) `infinity` serves **both** embeddings (mandatory) and the reranker (optional leg), so the probe is classified REQUIRED and the optional rerank leg reports `RERANK_UNAVAILABLE` at the leg level; (b) `llm_gateway` is REQUIRED for `ask` only and `UNUSED` for `search` — matching the readiness.py decision not to probe the billable gateway, and giving T-V1-026 a registered code to fail closed with.

## 2026-10-12 T-V1-026 (Traces + honest failure statuses per leg)
TASK: T-V1-026 (MICRO-TICKETS-V1, trace RP-11 / QF-11, depends T-V1-025 landed at `39e8036`)
CHANGE:
  - `packages/retrieval/orchestrator.py` — new `RetrievalStatus` (`ok`/`degraded`/`unavailable`), `LegFailure` (leg + exception **class name only** + canonical `ReasonCode`), `RetrievalResult`, `LEG_REASON_CODES`, `REQUIRED_LEGS`, `retrieval_outcome_ctx` + `pop_retrieval_outcome()`; `_dense_leg` split into an **embedding** step and a **dense-store** step so a failure names the dependency that broke; new `_lexical_leg`/`_graph_leg` runners all record failures on a per-call `_LegReport`; `retrieve_result()` is the rich API, `retrieve()` stays list-shaped **and** publishes the outcome to the context slot. +395/−115.
  - `apps/api/app/search/search.py` — `SearchResponse` gains `status`/`reason_code`/`reason`/`trace_id` (additive, defaulted); a **required** leg failing → **503** + registered safe reason + `trace_id`; degraded optional leg → 200 + `status: "degraded"`. +59.
  - `packages/answer/engine.py` — reads the outcome after `retrieve()`; required-leg-down → `AnswerStatus.UNAVAILABLE` (+ safe reason + tracking ID) **before** the model is called; optional-leg-down downgrades a standing answer to `degraded` via `_disclose()`; the generation leg logs `generation_leg_failed` with the tracking ID and re-raises. +149/−.
  - `apps/api/app/ask/router.py` — `AnswerStatus.UNAVAILABLE` → 503 with the registered status + `trace_id` (now uniform with /search); the existing `LLMProviderError` 503 gains `trace_id`. +30/−.
  - `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py` — `process_one` binds the job id to the correlation slot for the whole job; `record_failure` log lines carry `trace_id`. +20/−.
  - `tests/unit/test_orchestrator.py` — 3 tests that pinned the *old* silent-empty behavior re-pinned to the honest contract (see RISKS a); rerank test extended with the degraded disclosure. +58/−.
  - `tests/unit/test_leg_failure_statuses.py` — **NEW**, 32 tests: per-leg failure injection (embedding / dense retrieval / lexical retrieval / rerank / generation), empty-corpus-is-not-a-failure (unit + HTTP), no-silent-empty sweep across 4 modes, safe-trace lint, trace-context propagation + single-consumption, hydration-decorator passthrough, worker ingress→queue→worker binding, HTTP 503/200/degraded boundary for both /search and /ask, single-registry lock.
WHY: RP-11/QF-11 — "no results" was indistinguishable from "search is down". Every leg failure was logged and then **converted into an empty list**, so an embeddings/Qdrant/Postgres outage surfaced as a 200 with `results: []` (search) or, worse, the engine still **called the model on an unsearched corpus** and reported `insufficient_evidence` (ask). DECISIONS row: **fail closed on REQUIRED legs, degrade on optional ones** — grounded in T-V1-025's `DEPENDENCY_MATRIX`, whose handoff explicitly left `REQUIRED` as "data, not enforcement… the matrix gives T-V1-026 the verdict + reason code to do so". This ticket is that enforcement. The status-level reason for a degraded run is deliberately kept to `RERANK_UNAVAILABLE`/`DEGRADED_RESULT` so it always satisfies `ANSWER_STATUS_REASON_CODES[degraded]`; per-leg codes (`GRAPH_UNAVAILABLE`, …) stay on `legs_failed`.
REUSED: no new dependency, no vendor code. Reused `core.errors` (T-V1-025 taxonomy) for every code/status/HTTP mapping and `core.logging.request_id_ctx` (pre-existing) as the correlation slot — this ticket adds **zero** reason codes and **zero** parallel taxonomies (locked by test).
BUILT: `RetrievalResult`/`LegFailure`/`RetrievalStatus` and the context-local outcome slot, because `retrieve()`'s frozen `list[RetrievedItem]` signature is pinned by the M3-004 contract tests and by ~6 call sites (search, ask-via-hydration, eval). Changing the return type would have broken all of them; the context slot lets **every** existing caller — including `HydratingOrchestrator` (T-V1-022), whose `__getattr__` would otherwise have forwarded `retrieve_result` to the *unhydrated* inner orchestrator — read the honest verdict with no signature change and no hydration bypass. Chose the slot over a new method for exactly that reason.
VERIFY (repo root `Super Rag/knowledge-os`):
- Ticket gate → `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest -q tests/unit/test_leg_failure_statuses.py` → **`32 passed`**.
- Regression gate → `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest -q tests/unit tests/contracts tests/integration/test_ask_route.py` → **`872 passed`** (start-of-ticket baseline for unit+contracts was 840; +32 = my new file, **0 regressions**).
- Lint/type → `ruff check packages/retrieval/orchestrator.py packages/answer/engine.py apps/api/app/search/search.py apps/api/app/ask/router.py apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py tests/unit/test_leg_failure_statuses.py tests/unit/test_orchestrator.py` → **`All checks passed!`**; `mypy` on the 5 product files → **`Success: no issues found in 5 source files`**.
- **Mutation controls (not vacuous)** — (a) disable the required-leg fail-closed return → **12 failed / 20 passed**; (b) drop `retrieval_outcome_ctx.set(result)` from `retrieve()` → **6 failed / 26 passed**; (c) neuter the search route's 503 branch → **1 failed / 31 passed**; all restored → **32 passed**.
- **Metrics** — legs with silent-empty conversion: **0** (every mode covered by the parametrized sweep + `test_silent_leg_failure_leaves_no_trace`); injected failures with tracking IDs: **4/4** retrieval legs + generation (embedding, dense, lexical, rerank, and the LLM gateway); empty-corpus returns empty: **yes**, at unit level (`status=ok`, `items=[]`, `reason_code=None`) and at the HTTP boundary (200 on both /search and /ask); surfaced traces carrying exception text/SQL/hostnames/prompt content: **0** (class name + registry message only, lint-locked).
REVIEWER CHECKLIST: **(1) empty ≠ failed everywhere** — an empty corpus is `ok` + `[]` (unit + HTTP on both routes); an empty/unscoped ACL scope and a blank query are also `ok` (no attempt was made, so they can never read as an outage); only a *failed* required leg is `unavailable`. **(2) tracking IDs present** — every failure path carries `trace_id`, derived from the request's `x-request-id` (API) or the job id (worker), so ingress→queue→worker→stores is one joinable trace with no schema change. **(3) no prompt/secret content in traces** — `LegFailure.error_type` is the exception class name only (never `str(exc)`), reasons come from the registry's safe messages, and a lint test bans `http(s)://`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `traceback`, `select ` plus the injected exception text.
RISKS:
  (a) **Deliberate behavior change on 3 pre-existing tests — flagged for the reviewer, not smuggled.** `test_missing_leg_degrades`, `test_semantic_mode_degrades_to_empty_on_dense_failure` and `test_keyword_mode_degrades_to_empty_on_lexical_failure` previously pinned that a broken leg degrades to `[]` and that a surviving leg's results are still served. T-V1-026 instruction 1 ("stop converting leg failures to silent empty results") and its metric ("legs with silent-empty conversion: 0") mandate the opposite, so all three were re-pinned to the honest contract with **stronger** assertions (status + reason code + tracking id + failed-leg identity, not just a log-line match). No test was weakened or deleted.
  (b) **Availability trade-off, the main product judgement.** A single required leg failing now fails the whole search/ask closed, even when another leg still worked (e.g. Qdrant up, Postgres FTS down ⇒ 503). Rationale: the V1 gate is Recall@20 ≥95% and grounded ≥90%, and answering from a silently-partial corpus is exactly the failure class RP-11/QF-11 exists to kill. If the coordinator prefers partial availability, the switch is one line (`REQUIRED_LEGS`) plus a disclosure contract — but that is a **new ticket**, not a tweak, because the UI would then have to render "partial corpus" honestly.
  (c) **`graph` mode never fails closed.** `REQUIRED_LEGS[graph] == ()`, so a graph-leg outage degrades rather than blocks — grounded in `DEPENDENCY_MATRIX` classifying `graph_store` OPTIONAL for search/ask. Disclosed rather than hidden; V2 may revisit when the graph leg becomes a primary evidence source.
  (d) **`search.py` gained response fields (additive, defaulted).** `status`/`reason_code`/`reason`/`trace_id` are new optional keys on `SearchResponse`. No TS change was needed (additive keys never break `tsc`, and `contracts.ts` mirrors only the enums, which did not move). Flagging because `apps/web` is outside my allowlist and was therefore not touched.
  (e) **`retrieve()` publishes to a context slot, so it is per-task, not per-object.** Correct under asyncio (contextvars are task-local, so concurrent requests never read each other's outcome — `pop_retrieval_outcome()` also clears it, so nothing goes stale). A caller that spawns `retrieve()` in a *child task* and reads the outcome in the parent would see nothing; no such caller exists today (all 6 are direct `await`s), and the healthy default keeps that case merely un-informative, never wrong.
  (f) **Two pre-existing integration failures, NOT mine.** `tests/integration/test_ingestion_jobs.py::test_pipeline_reaches_ready_with_chunks` and `::test_charset_hint_round_trip` fail — proven pre-existing by re-running them against `git show HEAD:apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py` (same 2 failures). They come from the foreign, uncommitted-at-my-start T-V1-019 work in `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` (root cause recorded upstream: `count_ids` AttributeError / T-V1-042). That file was **never opened or edited by me**.
  (g) **No commit / no deploy / no service restart / nothing staged by me.**

## 2026-10-12 T-V1-027 (`/ready` checks what the mode actually needs)

TASK: T-V1-027 (MICRO-TICKETS-V1, trace RP-11 / QF-11; depends T-V1-005 `0cc7048` + T-V1-025 `39e8036`, both landed; repo pinned @ `f2e29cd`, HEAD unmoved for the whole ticket)
CHANGE:
  - `apps/api/app/adapters/readiness.py` (+414/−) — probe set is now derived from the *enabled modes*: new `GRAPH_MODES_ON`, `BASE_DEPENDENCIES` (7 names), `graph_probe_state()` (`enabled`/`off`/`invalid`), `probe_names(graph_mode)` (the pin target), `enabled_modes()` + `required_dependencies_for_modes()` (union of `core.errors.required_dependencies()` over search/ask/ingest + graph-when-on); three new capability probes — `_ModelGatewayProbe` (`GET {LITELLM_URL}{READINESS_MODEL_HEALTH_PATH}`, sub-500 rule, empty URL ⇒ fail-closed red), `_WorkerConsumerProbe` (Redis `CLIENT LIST` must show a `BRPOP` consumer), `_GraphStoreProbe` (sqlite file readable, or parent creatable per `sqlite_store.py:92` `mkdir`, else red); `_unprobed()` (a REQUIRED dep with no probe ⇒ red, never silent-green); `_invalid_graph_mode()` (a typo'd `GRAPH_MODE` still reports `graph_store`, red, instead of dropping it); `_gather_fresh()` — tasks run concurrently under `asyncio.wait(..., timeout=freshness)`, anything still running is cancelled and reported red with a `freshness:` error (no result older than the bound is ever reported), and results are never cached between calls. Module docstring documents the exact set, the timeouts/freshness semantics, the "only REQUIRED deps are in the map ⇒ `all(reachable)` == required-down-means-not-ready" invariant and the review1-F3 rationale for probing the gateway.
  - `packages/core/config.py` (+25) — new `litellm_url` (default `http://localhost:4000`, = `models.adapters.litellm_provider.DEFAULT_BASE_URL`, so readiness and the provider agree on "no gateway configured" in a bare host), `readiness_model_health_path` (`/health/liveliness`), `graph_mode`/`graph_db_path` (same env vars `packages/graph/factory.py` reads), `readiness_capability_timeout_seconds` (2.0) and `readiness_freshness_seconds` (5.0); `readiness_timeout_seconds` (1.5) kept and commented as the store-probe budget.
  - `tests/fixtures/scripts/m004_ready_degraded.py` (+32/−) — pinned set `postgres, redis, qdrant, minio, infinity, llm_gateway, worker` (+`graph_store` iff GRAPH_MODE on, `off` here); adds `LITELLM_URL`→closed port, `GRAPH_MODE=off`, `READINESS_CAPABILITY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=1.5`, `READINESS_FRESHNESS_SECONDS=5.0`; elapsed message now cites the freshness budget.

  - `handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md` — this row.
WHY: ticket instructions 1–2. (1) "Include embeddings/reranker/model/worker (+graph when enabled) with timeouts + freshness" — the set is the union of `DEPENDENCY_MATRIX`'s REQUIRED deps over the enabled modes (T-V1-025's single source of truth, no second taxonomy): `infinity` is embeddings **and** the reranker (PD-002: one service ⇒ one probe; the rerank leg is OPTIONAL and degrades at leg level with `RERANK_UNAVAILABLE`), `llm_gateway` is the model, `worker` the queue consumer, `graph_store` only when `GRAPH_MODE` is `rule`/`llm`. Timeouts = per-probe budgets (1.5s stores / 2.0s capabilities) + an outer freshness bound; freshness = "no reported result may be older than the bound, and nothing is cached between calls" — a success cache would have allowed ready-during-outage for up to the TTL, which the ticket's metric forbids, so the bound cuts the other way (stale ⇒ red). (2) "exact-set degraded contract in all 3 pinned places" — the identical 3-line expression now lives in fixture + both tests, and each asserts **equality**, not a subset. "required-down ⇒ not-ready" needed no router change: `health.py`'s `all(dep.reachable)` already decides `ready`, and because the map now contains only REQUIRED-for-enabled-mode deps that verdict is exact (an optional dep is disclosed in the docstring, never silently green).
REUSED: zero new dependencies (`pyproject.toml`/`package.json` untouched). Reused `core.errors.{Dependency, ServiceMode, DEPENDENCY_MATRIX, required_dependencies}` from T-V1-025 as the classification, the existing probe modules + `core.health.DependencyStatus` shape, redis-py (already pinned) `client_list()`, httpx (already pinned), stdlib `asyncio`/`pathlib`/`os`. Probe names stay `Dependency` values, which is also what `tests/contracts/test_error_taxonomy_contract.py::test_readiness_probe_set_is_fully_classified` scans for — that contract now sees all 8 entries including the conditional `graph_store` and still reports `unclassified == []`.
BUILT: the three capability probes + the freshness watchdog (~150 lines) — no library fits: the worker has **no HTTP surface and no heartbeat** (M0 entrypoint blocks on `BRPOP`), so the only honest observable on this allowlist is the blocked consumer on its broker; the graph store is an embedded SQLite file with no server; the gateway check must be a bare unauthenticated health GET (F3: no billable completion on a readiness poll). Reusing a generic HTTP client would not have supplied the consumer/graph semantics, and adding a worker heartbeat would need `apps/worker/*` (outside the allowlist — see risks).
VERIFY (repo root `Super Rag/knowledge-os`; venv `/root/ko-venv`):

- Ticket gate → `pytest tests/integration/test_boot.py tests/integration/test_live_endpoints.py -q` → **`14 passed`** (13 pre-existing + my new contract test; 0 skipped — the strict all-reachable check passes for all 7).
- Ticket gate → `pytest tests/integration/test_landed_ready_degraded.py tests/contracts/test_error_taxonomy_contract.py -q` → **`16 passed`** (the landed m004 wrapper + the T-V1-025 taxonomy contract incl. the probe-set classification lock).
- Regression → `pytest tests/unit tests/contracts -q` → **`868 passed`**. `ruff check` on my 5 files → **`All checks passed!`**; `mypy apps/api/app/adapters/readiness.py packages/core/config.py` → **`Success: no issues found in 2 source files`**.
- Full integration → `pytest tests/integration -q` → `8 failed, 86 passed, 5 skipped, 2 xfailed`. **The 8 failures are pre-existing, proven:** my 2 product files were copied to `/tmp/opencode/tv1027/`, overwritten with `git show HEAD:…`, all 8 re-run → **the same 8 fail at baseline `f2e29cd`** (root cause: live `POST localhost:7997/embeddings` → `embedding_failed` / jobs stuck `queued`, the T-V1-043 dimension issue), then restored and `cmp`-verified byte-identical. None of them touches `/ready`.
- **Live stack (bind-mounted api container, no restart)** → `curl localhost:8000/ready` → `status=ok ready=True` with `postgres SELECT 1 ok`, `redis PING -> True`, `qdrant GET /readyz -> 200`, `minio GET / -> 200`, `infinity GET / -> 307`, **`llm_gateway GET /health/liveliness -> 404`, `worker CLIENT LIST -> 1 BRPOP consumer(s)`** — i.e. required-down is not reached only because every required capability is up.
- Evidence probes (OUTSIDE the repo, `/tmp/opencode/tv1027/probe_readiness.py`, one process per case): `freshness` → `PROBE_OK` (bound 0.05s ⇒ 6/7 checks cut with `freshness: no result within 0.05s`, `elapsed=0.29s`, `status=degraded ready=False`, exact set == `probe_names()`); `graph_on` → `PROBE_OK` (`graph_store` joins the set, green: `store ready, db created on first write`); `graph_invalid` (`GRAPH_MODE=banana`) → `PROBE_OK` (`graph_store` present, red: `not one of off/rule/llm (config error, fail closed)`); `graph_dir` (`GRAPH_DB_PATH=/etc/hostname/graph.db`) → `PROBE_OK` (red: `graph store directory not writable`).
- Hostile controls (`/tmp/opencode/tv1027/probe_controls.py`) → **`CONTROLS_OK`**: worker probe green with the real consumer, **red** (`no BRPOP consumer attached to the broker (worker not draining)`) for both an idle-client and an empty `CLIENT LIST` on the *same* broker URL — so the check bites and is not a Redis re-check in disguise; model probe `200 → green`, `500/503 → red`, refused → red, empty `LITELLM_URL` → red (`fail closed`).
METRICS: required deps checked across all enabled modes: **7/7 base + `graph_store` when graph is on** (union of REQUIRED over search/ask/ingest/graph == exactly the reported set, asserted in `test_boot`); ready-during-outage: **never** (all-down ⇒ `ready=false` proven by m004; nothing cached between calls; the freshness bound turns a hung probe into red, not into a stale green); checks with an unbounded timeout: **0** (1.5s / 2.0s / 5.0s, m004 asserts `/ready` ≤ 7.5s); dep-set drift between the 3 pinned places: **0** (identical expression + equality assertions + `probe_names` pin).
REVIEWER CHECKLIST: **(1) dep set identical in all 3 places** — the same `BASE_DEPENDENCIES` (7) + `GRAPH_ENABLED` + `DEPENDENCIES = BASE + (("graph_store",) if GRAPH_ENABLED else ())` in `m004_ready_degraded.py`, `test_boot.py`, `test_live_endpoints.py`; m004 asserts set equality against the body, test_boot asserts set equality **and** `tuple(body["dependencies"]) == probe_names(...)`, test_live asserts set equality, and test_boot pins `probe_names()` for `off`/`""`/`rule`/`llm`/`banana` so implementation and pins cannot drift apart. **(2) timeouts bounded** — per-probe transport budgets (1.5s stores, 2.0s capabilities) plus `READINESS_FRESHNESS_SECONDS` as a hard outer bound implemented with `asyncio.wait`+cancel, with the cut reported as an explicit `freshness:` error; m004 still asserts total `/ready` ≤ 7.5s. **(3) optional fallback disclosed** — module docstring: reranker rides on `infinity` (one service, one probe, optional leg degrades with `RERANK_UNAVAILABLE`), `graph_store` absent when graph is off, invalid `GRAPH_MODE` reported red rather than dropped, worker attribution limited to "a `BRPOP` consumer on that broker", and the F3 rationale for the gateway probe (health path only, no key, no completion, never cached).
RISKS:
  (a) **The freshness semantics are a design call.** I read "timeouts + freshness" as *max age of a reported result + re-probe every call* (a cached success would let `/ready` claim ready during an outage for up to the TTL — rejected for the ticket's metric). If the coordinator meant a result cache with a TTL, that is the one thing to re-review; it is a small change inside `_gather_fresh`.
  (b) **`/ready` now makes one outbound GET per call to the configured gateway** (health path, no API key, bounded 2s). Against the compose default (`https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`) that is real egress and a flappy external network can turn `/ready` red — it skips loudly in tests rather than passing falsely. F3's cost objection is addressed (no completion/tokens) but its latency/flapping objection is only *bounded*, not eliminated; `LITELLM_URL`/`READINESS_MODEL_HEALTH_PATH` are the knobs (empty URL ⇒ explicit fail-closed red, not silent green).
  (c) **Sub-500 rule on the gateway** (same rule T-V1-005 landed for infinity): a gateway that answers 404 — OpenRouter does — reads green, and so would any wrong-but-listening URL. Disclosed in the docstring; a real health contract would need F4's gateway topology settled.
  (d) **Worker check attributes a blocked `BRPOP` client, not a PID**, so a second worker or a queue consumer in a test also satisfies it; the alternative (broker-only) would report a dead worker as ready, which is the exact failure this ticket forbids. The durable fix is a worker heartbeat key (TTL-extended on each loop) — needs `apps/worker/*`, outside this allowlist; flagged as follow-up.
  (e) **`tests/integration/test_boot.py` now `setdefault`s `LITELLM_URL`** to the documented default so its strict "compose up ⇒ all reachable" gate still runs instead of skipping on an unset var; consequence is one external GET in that test (skips loudly with the `llm_gateway` detail if the runner has no egress). This also leaks into the rest of the pytest session — audited: no test asserts a `LiteLLMProvider` default base URL (all pass `base_url=` explicitly or use `http://127.0.0.1:1`), and `pytest tests/unit tests/contracts` stays 868 green.
  (f) **Graph probe reads the filesystem** (16 bytes of an existing file, or writability of the nearest existing ancestor) and never opens/migrates a live database; "parent creatable" mirrors what `sqlite_store.py` does on first write, so a fresh deployment reads green rather than false-red.
  (g) **8 pre-existing integration failures** (ingestion/embedding, `T-V1-043` dimension issue) remain red at baseline — proven above; none is caused by or fixed here. Foreign churn during the ticket (`M handoffs/MUSER-LOOP-STATE.md`, `M packages/ingestion/adapters/redis_queue.py` +203 — parallel dev1) was observed and **not touched**. No commit / no deploy / no service restart / nothing staged by me.

CHANGE (8 paths, ALL inside the allowed list — 3 product files + 4 updated test files + 1 new test file + this handoff row; zero foreign product paths):
  - `packages/models/router_config.py` — the policy module now OWNS the full contract. (1) Adds `PRECEDENCE_ORDER`, the single authoritative LOCKED order (`veto > permitted_override > workspace_rules > operator_default`), with the T-V1-038 discrepancy reconciled in the module docstring. (2) Adds `route_decision(query_class, config) -> (model, reason)` — model and reason returned in ONE policy pass so the recorded reason can never disagree with the pick; `route()` is now a thin wrapper over it (byte-compatible return). (3) Adds `load_workspace_config(workspace_id)` — the authoritative per-workspace loader reading the `WORKSPACE_MODEL_CONFIG` env JSON store (absent/unparseable/other-workspace ⇒ `None`, malformed rules skipped not crashed). (4) Adds `provider_identity(model)` — non-secret logical provider name (`litellm`|`openrouter`), never host/URL/credential.
  - `apps/api/app/ask/router.py` — `load_model_config()` now CALLS `load_workspace_config()` so the workspace tier actually loads authoritative config instead of the old empty stub (`routing_rules=()` hardcoded). `resolve_model()` delegates to `route_decision()` (drops the old re-derivation hack `_rules_selected()` + the now-unused `replace` import). Handler logs `provider` (via `provider_identity`) alongside `model`/`reason`. The router's pick is threaded into `build_answer_engine(model=resolved_model)` — the post-construction `engine.model = resolved_model` overwrite is GONE (single source, no second assignment).
  - `packages/answer/factory.py` — REMOVED the `gpt-4o-mini` silent default: `DEFAULT_ANSWER_MODEL` constant deleted, `os.environ.get("ANSWER_MODEL", ...)` deleted, and `build_answer_engine(*, model: str, ...)` now takes a REQUIRED explicit model. The factory holds no default and reads no model env var; the policy router is the only model source.
  - `tests/unit/test_router_precedence_lock.py` (NEW, 18 tests) — pins the LOCKED order, override-above-rules, veto supremacy, `route()==route_decision()[0]` consistency, workspace-config loads/flows/degrades (absent+unparseable+other-ws), all 3 precedence levels through the REAL handler, provider identity (non-secret), and a no-secrets log assertion (plants `LITELLM_API_KEY=sk-...` and asserts it + query text never reach any record). Plus a factory AST-based source test (asserts on CODE, not prose) banning any default model.
  - `tests/unit/test_ask_query_class.py` — `_ask` fake factory now captures the REQUIRED `model=` kwarg; source-pin test updated to the new contract (`build_answer_engine(model=resolved_model)` == 1, `engine.model =` absent). 16 tests still pass.
  - `tests/integration/test_ask_route.py` (+2), `tests/unit/test_leg_failure_statuses.py` (+2), `tests/integration/test_security_placeholders.py` (+1) — fake `build_answer_engine` lambdas updated to `lambda **_: ...` for the new required-`model` signature. Behavior-neutral.
WHY: The ticket's goal — "the configured default wins; overrides follow documented precedence" — plus the coordinator ruling **"override-above-rules stays"** that RESOLVES the T-V1-038 discrepancy note (see that row's risk (a): it asked for a ruling on whether to reorder to workspace-above-override). Ruling received: keep the landed order (override ABOVE workspace rules) and LOCK it explicitly. DECISIONS/M7-001a: precedence stays owned by `models.router_config`; this ticket moves the reason-derivation INTO the policy (`route_decision`) so call sites report, never decide. Reconciles MICRO-TICKETS-V1 wording ("workspace > override > default") by documenting that "workspace" = the authoritative config SOURCE that LOADS, not a tier that outranks the caller's permitted override.
REUSED: none (no new dependency, no vendored code). Reuses existing `WorkspaceModelConfig`/`RoutingRule`/`MODEL_REGISTRY` and stdlib `json`/`logging`. No `REUSE_MAP.md` addition needed.
BUILT: `route_decision` (single-pass model+reason), `load_workspace_config` (env-JSON authoritative loader), `provider_identity`, `PRECEDENCE_ORDER` — all core-product glue that no permissive-FOSS lib provides; each sits inside the existing adapter/policy boundary, no vendor SDK in business logic.

Silent overrides: **0** (factory default deleted; source tests ban `gpt-4o-mini`/`DEFAULT_ANSWER_MODEL` in both handler and factory CODE). Reason+provider logged per request: **1 event**, reason 5-value enum, provider ∈ {litellm, openrouter}; **prompt content and planted credential in log records: 0** (asserted over every record + extras). Precedence order LOCKED by `PRECEDENCE_ORDER` + pin test. Product files outside allowlist: **0**.

RISKS: medium-low.

In-tree all callers/fakes were updated (5 test files); if a downstream branch calls it zero-arg it must now pass `model=`. This is intentional (the whole point is "no silent default").

Deliberate: a bad store should not take Ask down, and it is logged (`workspace_model_config_unparseable`). If museR wants fail-closed, that is a one-line change in `load_workspace_config`.

It does not introspect the live `LiteLLMProvider` base_url (that would leak host/env coupling into the policy); it is an observability label, not a routing input. `litellm_provider.py` itself was NOT modified (no change needed).

## 2026-10-12 T-V1-029 (local-only means local or explicit unavailable)

TASK: T-V1-029 — MICRO-TICKETS-V1 §"Models" (S, ~20 min). Trace: RP-16, QF-16. Depends T-V1-028, landed at `ea8ce26` = the ticket pin (HEAD moved to `b0de45f` mid-ticket by a parallel T-V1-020 commit; `git diff ea8ce26 b0de45f -- <my paths>` is EMPTY, so none of my work was touched or clobbered). The ticket carries no explicit allowlist line; I inherited T-V1-028's (`apps/api/app/ask/router.py` + `packages/answer/factory.py` + `packages/models/router_config.py` + `packages/models/adapters/litellm_provider.py` + tests) — exactly where the local-only path lives — and stayed inside it (disclosed inference, flagged in RISKS h).
CHANGE (9 paths: 4 product + 4 test + this row; zero foreign product paths):
  - `packages/models/router_config.py` (+162/−) — the policy now OWNS the local-only contract. (1) `LOCAL_ONLY_SENTINEL = "local-only"` is re-defined as what it always was on the wire — a POLICY VERDICT label, never a model name: `route_decision` no longer returns it; the veto tier resolves to `configured_local_model()` or raises `LocalOnlyUnavailable` (fixed safe message "no local model is configured for this local-only workspace"). (2) Explicit-local resolution: `LOCAL_ONLY_MODEL_ENV` (`LOCAL_ONLY_MODEL`, operator declaration, wins) → cheapest `local=True` `MODEL_REGISTRY` profile → `None` = fail closed; workspace `extra_models` deliberately do NOT qualify (transport locality is operator-level config, and the factory seam cannot see per-workspace config — the gap is closed by design, not papered over). (3) Transport locality is policy data: `LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL_ENV` (`LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL`, default loopback `http://127.0.0.1:4000`), `is_local_endpoint()` (loopback `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1`, plus an explicit `LOCAL_GATEWAY_EXTRA_HOSTS` allowlist — everything else is egress), `local_gateway_url()`, `is_local_model()`. (4) `provider_identity()` gains `PROVIDER_LOCAL` — a local model logs `provider=local`. (5) `load_workspace_config()` reads the store's `"sensitive": true` (strict JSON boolean; a string is not an opt-in) so a workspace can actually BE local-only in production; the veto was previously reachable only from unit tests.
  - `packages/models/adapters/litellm_provider.py` (+43/−) — the EGRESS-POINT backstop (reviewer checklist 1): new `_guard_model_name()` refuses the sentinel in BOTH `generate()` and `stream()` before any payload/client is touched (`LLMProviderError`, zero transport invocations) — even a caller that bypasses the policy entirely cannot post `local-only` to ANY gateway, external or local; and a new `require_local=True` constructor flag fails closed (`LLMProviderError`) when the resolved base_url is not an explicitly local endpoint — checked at construction, before a client exists.
  - `packages/answer/factory.py` (+27/−) — `_llm_provider_for(model)`: a model the policy declares local (`is_local_model`) is built ONLY on the local transport (`LiteLLMProvider(base_url=local_gateway_url(), require_local=True)`), bypassing the `LITELLM_URL`/`OPENROUTER_API_KEY` default resolution entirely; cloud models keep the existing defaults untouched. Signature UNCHANGED (`build_answer_engine(*, model, collection)`) — the transport follows the model's policy classification, so no call site, no fake and no T-V1-028 source pin had to move.
  - `apps/api/app/ask/router.py` (+33/−) — `load_model_config` threads `sensitive` from the loaded store entry; the handler wraps its SINGLE `resolve_model(...)` call in `try/except LocalOnlyUnavailable` → **503** + `error_detail(ReasonCode.LLM_UNAVAILABLE, str(exc))` (registered wire `llm_unavailable`) + `trace_id` — raised BEFORE `build_answer_engine`, so no engine (hence no gateway client) exists on the fail-closed path. `resolved_model, reason = resolve_model(` count == 1 and `build_answer_engine(model=resolved_model)` count == 1 source pins UNTOUCHED.
  - `tests/unit/test_local_only_no_egress.py` (**NEW, 17 tests**, the sensitive fixture): policy (fail-closed sweep × 4 query classes with the exact safe message; veto resolves to the declared local model and still beats override/rules/default; sentinel-never-routable sweep across every config class; resolution order incl. deterministic tie-break; endpoint classification incl. allowlist-is-not-a-wildcard); provider (`_RecordingClient` dummy — sentinel handed DIRECTLY → generate+stream raise with `posts == []` and `streams == []` against BOTH an external and a local base_url; `require_local` construction refusal incl. the env path; a local request posts to exactly the loopback URL); factory (local model → loopback provider EVEN with `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`+`LITELLM_URL` planted external; non-local `LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL` → `LLMProviderError` at build, no provider; cloud model → cloud defaults unchanged); REAL handler (`WORKSPACE_MODEL_CONFIG` store marks the workspace `sensitive`, external gateway config planted in every case): case A → 503/`llm_unavailable`/safe message/`trace_id` with the factory recorder seeing **0 builds** (nothing could invoke a gateway), case B → the engine is built with the local model (≠ sentinel), log event `provider=local reason=policy_veto_local_only`; plus store-`sensitive` wiring (strict boolean) and provider-identity-no-secret pins. Every HTTP client is in-memory; no real network in any test.
  - `tests/unit/test_router_config.py` (+36/−), `tests/unit/test_router_precedence_lock.py` (+23/−), `tests/unit/test_ask_query_class.py` (+7/−) — RE-PINS (flagged, not smuggled): `test_veto_never_overridden` asserted `route(...) == "local-only"` for 3 query classes and `test_veto_beats_everything_and_is_never_overridden` asserted `route_decision(...) == ("local-only", ...)` — the exact contract T-V1-029 forbids ("sentinel label alone is not routing"). Re-pinned with stronger assertions: fail-closed `pytest.raises(LocalOnlyUnavailable)` where nothing is configured, resolved local model + veto reason where something is; `test_ask_selection_reason_labels_the_veto_tier` keeps its intent (the tier is still labelled `policy_veto_local_only`) with the local model declared. No test was weakened or deleted.
WHY: ticket instructions 1–2 + reviewer checklist. The pre-T-V1-029 veto returned the literal string `"local-only"` as the MODEL, the handler threaded it into `build_answer_engine`, and `LiteLLMProvider` dutifully POSTed `{"model": "local-only", "messages": <sensitive prompt>}` to `{base_url}/chat/completions` — and with `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` set, `LiteLLMProvider.__init__` defaults base_url to `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`: a sensitive workspace's prompt would have been egressed to an external gateway with the local-only marker as the model id. The DECISIONS-consistent fix (one framework, adapter boundary): the policy RESOLVES the veto to an explicitly allowed local model or fails closed; the factory keys the transport to that classification; the provider refuses the sentinel at the egress point. `LLM_UNAVAILABLE` (existing registered 503 code, category DEPENDENCY) is the documented unavailable — adding a new `LOCAL_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` code would move `packages/core/errors.py` + the TS mirror + the taxonomy contract test, all outside the inherited allowlist, and the fixed safe message already distinguishes the case on the wire (flagged as an optional follow-up in RISKS i).
REUSED: zero new dependencies (`pyproject.toml`/`package.json` untouched). Reused `core.errors` (`ReasonCode.LLM_UNAVAILABLE`/`error_detail`), stdlib `urllib.parse.urlparse` for endpoint classification (no DNS, no network), the existing `LiteLLMProvider` OpenAI-compatible transport (which `packages/models/interfaces.py` already documents as the seam for "local vLLM / Ollama / llama.cpp servers via their OpenAI-compatible endpoints" — this ticket is that docstring made enforceable), httpx's injectable client seam for dummy transports. No REUSE_MAP/NOTICES delta.
BUILT: `configured_local_model`/`is_local_model`/`is_local_endpoint`/`local_gateway_url` + `LocalOnlyUnavailable` (~120 lines of policy) — nothing in-tree or upstream classifies an endpoint as local vs egress or binds a model identity to a transport class, and no FOSS lib can: it is a deployment-policy decision (loopback vs allowlisted LAN host vs public), and the whole point is that the classification lives in OUR policy layer, not in an HTTP client. The provider's two guards are the adapter-boundary expression of that policy at the only place egress can happen.

If per-workspace local models are ever needed, the resolution must move the whole (model, transport) pair through one seam — a new ticket, not a tweak.
  (b) **"Local" = loopback or explicit allowlist.** `LOCAL_GATEWAY_EXTRA_HOSTS` exists for a LAN vLLM, but by default a compose-internal hostname (e.g. `http://litellm:4000`) is treated as egress and fails closed — honest and safe, but an operator running a sensitive workspace behind a docker-internal hostname MUST set the allowlist (or use loopback); otherwise they get the documented 503, never a silent leak.

  (e) **The handler-level sensitive fixture asserts the seam, not a live local model.** Case B records the engine build with the local model and `provider=local`; that the engine for such a model can ONLY be a loopback provider is pinned separately at the factory/provider level (tests 9–11 + mutations iv). Composition is by construction (factory keys off `is_local_model`, the same function the policy resolved with); an end-to-end HTTP call to a live Ollama is out of scope ("dummy transports only" per the ticket's Tests line).

A log-parsing dashboard expecting only the two old values would see `local` on sensitive workspaces — disclosed.

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-024 (RO/PT negation preserves commitment)

TASK: T-V1-024 (MICRO-TICKETS-V1 §D, trace RP-08 / QF-08; depends T-V1-022 boundary — landed; ticket pin `c8994b2`)
CHANGE:
  - packages/answer/verification.py (+318/−60) — `ExtractiveGrounder`'s commitment check becomes **language-aware (EN/RO/PT)**, staying inside the ticket's allowed file and the pre-existing architecture (closed marker classes + structural frames, no new dependency, no interface change; `ClaimGrounder`/`verify_claims` untouched):
    1. **One closed commitment class per language.** The single EN-only `_NEGATION` / `_CONDITIONAL` / `_REPORTING_VERB` become `_NEGATION_{EN,RO,PT}`, `_CONDITIONAL_{EN,RO,PT}`, `_REPORTING_VERB_{EN,RO,PT}` (EN vocabulary byte-identical; `_NEGATION`/`_CONDITIONAL`/`_REPORTING_VERB` kept as backward-compatible aliases). RO/PT get their own negation ("nu / nu este adevărat", "não / não é verdade / falso"), conditional ("dacă / cu condiția", "se / caso / desde que") and reporting-verb ("a declarat / a spus / susține / potrivit", "afirmou / disse / segundo / de acordo com") vocabularies — this is the **reported bug**: the EN-only classes never fired, so `Alice a declarat că bugetul este de 900 de dolari.` / `Alice afirmou que o orçamento é de 900 dólares.` were grounded as the source's own fact.
    2. **`_markers_for()` routes per sentence.** A cheap closed hint (`_RO_HINT`/`_PT_HINT`: diacritics + unmistakable function words only; ASCII words that are also English — "in", "as", "no" — deliberately excluded) picks the class set; RO and PT get the **union** of both Romance sets, so a hint that cannot separate Romanian from Portuguese can only cost recall, never correctness. `_is_attributed_frame()` now takes the routed verb class (default = EN, so existing callers are unchanged) and `_SUBORDINATOR`/`_QUOTE_CHARS` gained RO/PT subordinators and „ " « » quotes.
    3. **Conservative abstain for the two languages we cannot parse** (`_is_whole_sentence_assertion`, the ticket's explicit "OR conservative abstain"): for an RO/PT sentence the claim must *be* the whole sentence's assertion, so every construction the interim grounder cannot positively model — negation, conditionals, reported speech, qualified quantities — arrives as a sentence fragment and abstains. Disclosed as a limitation in the class docstring (recall given up, never correctness).
    4. **Quantities/units** (`_APPROXIMATION`, closed EN+RO+PT class, applied only to digit-bearing claims so prose like "evidence **about** permissions" is out of scope) + **locale numeric guards**: the claim's numeric boundary is now `(?<![0-9.,])…\b(?![.,][0-9])`, closing two real partial-value holes — "900 dollars" matching inside "**1,**900 dollars" (the lookbehind only blocked `.`/digits, and `,` is a word boundary) and "900" matching the head of "900**,**5".
    5. Docstrings (module + class) updated: T-V1-024 behaviour, the no-keyword-counting property, and the RO/PT whole-sentence limitation added to the disclosed-Limitations list.
  - tests/unit/test_answer_engine.py (+215/−0, **APPEND only**) — the ticket's 12-case fixture + 5 supporting tests (41 new tests): `_T024_FALSE_FRAMES` (6 = 2 per language: clause-level negation + reported speech), `_T024_POSITIVES` (6 = 2 per language), a fixture-shape test that *pins* 6/6 + 2-per-language, grounder+`verify_claims` and engine-e2e variants of both, a 10-case unsupported-construction table (conditionals, quantity hedges, `1,900`/`900,5` partials, RO/PT fronted attribution), a no-keyword-counting table (token-identical role swaps + 8/9-overlap quantity changes), and the disclosed cross-language test.
WHY: spec goal "'It is false that the budget is 900' must never support 'budget is 900' — in any language" + instruction "language-aware context-preserving check (approved adapter) OR conservative abstain on negation/conditionals/reported-speech/quantities". I implemented BOTH halves: closed per-language classes (the context-preserving check) plus the RO/PT whole-sentence abstain (the conservative half, since v1 has no RO/PT clause parser). Satisfies the ticket metric verbatim: **false-frame rejections 6/6 (2 per language), positive passes 6/6 (2 per language)**. Reviewer checklist **(1) RO+PT false frames reject** ✅ (both RO and PT negation AND reported-speech budget-900 frames abstain at grounder, `verify_claims` and engine level), **(2) positives still pass** ✅ (6/6, including multi-sentence RO/PT chunks, each with a source-relative verbatim span), **(3) no keyword-counting pseudo-entailment** ✅ (nothing scores overlap: containment + closed markers, and token-identical/8/9-overlap contradictions abstain).
REUSED: none (no new deps, no new files). Reuses the module's existing design — closed marker classes, the 4 structural attribution frames, `_SENTENCE_BOUNDARY`, `re.IGNORECASE`-on-original-text span computation — extended per language; `_NEGATION`/`_CONDITIONAL`/`_REPORTING_VERB` names and 2-arg `_is_attributed_frame` calls keep working (aliases/defaults).

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-035 (sync convergence proof)

TASK: T-V1-035 — MICRO-TICKETS-V1 §F (M, ~30 min). Trace: RP-14, QF-14. Depends T-V1-034 (landed at `c86cbfb`, unreviewed — this ticket reads its handoff row for seams). **Test + fixtures only** — the ticket allows "new convergence test + fixtures (no product code unless T-034 left gaps — report, do NOT freelance)". T-034 left **no gap** for anything this ticket asks for: every guard the fixtures exercise is already in `sync_consumer.py`/the adapters, so **zero product files touched**. Ticket pin `c86cbfb` = HEAD, **UNMOVED** the whole ticket.
CHANGE (2 paths, both allowed — new test file + this row; **zero product, zero foreign**):
  - `tests/unit/test_sync_convergence.py` (**NEW, 12 tests**) — the convergence suite. A deterministic harness (no network, no services, fixed-seed shuffle, no sleeps) drives the **real** `QueuedSyncConsumer` through three seams: a controllable in-memory `Source` (create/update/delete/shuffle) behind a `SourceConnector` that uses the **production** `page_items`/`snapshot_digest` (not a re-implementation); a versioned `PublishedStore` behind the T-034 `publisher` seam (a *published version* = a new `(id, checksum)` row only when the checksum differs from the head, so re-publishing the same checksum is idempotent — mirroring a real versioned store, while every invocation is logged so at-least-once redelivery is observable); and a `LeaseQueue` modelling T-V1-020 claim/ack/**lease redelivery** (a raised `run_once` leaves the claim un-acked → `nack` → redelivered). The 5 scenarios + controls:
    1. **create** — full run creates every item exactly once (4/4, one version each, correct live set).
    2. **update** — 2 changed items append exactly **one** new version each, the rest `unchanged`, no duplicate checksums.
    3. **delete** — a deleted item is never fetched / re-published / resurrected; the live set drops to the surviving ids. (Tombstoning the already-published *document* — dropping its vectors/rows — is ingestion's T-V1-017, disclosed, not this seam's.)
    4. **reorder** — perturbing the source's stored order changes nothing: same converged head, `unchanged==5`, zero extra publishes (the connector serves key order, so the keyset cursor is order-independent).
    5. **retry** (= **kill mid-sync → resume**) — the publisher dies on `c.md` mid-run, the claim is un-acked, the lease **redelivers once**, and the resumed run finishes **exactly-once** with the correct set and no duplicates. This is the ticket's "kill mid-sync, resume, correct set, no duplicate published versions" through the real claim/ack seam.
    6. **interrupt mid-page → resume does NOT re-publish an already-outcomed sibling** (the exactly-once crux): `page_size=2` → pages `[a,b]` then `[c,d]`; the publisher dies on `d` *after* `c` was outcome-recorded but *before* page 2's cursor was saved. On resume the cursor is still `k:b`, so `[c,d]` is re-served — `c` is re-classified `unchanged` (already outcome-recorded) and **not** re-published (count stays 1), `d` published exactly once.
    7. **reorder + interrupt-resume compose** — crash at `k:b`, shuffle the source order, resume → keyset still serves exactly `c,d`; converges exactly-once (reviewer checklist 1+2 together).
    8. **failed item visible + retryable** — a transient read failure yields a `failed` outcome with the **canonical T-V1-025 code** (`ingestion_error`, round-trips through `canonical_code`), the healthy item is unaffected, and the next run retries the failed item to convergence.
    9. **unauthorized path excluded + visible** — the **real `LocalFolderConnector`** on a symlink that escapes the root: the item is discovered but `fetch` refuses it (`path escapes root`), it surfaces as a visible `failed` outcome, and it **never reaches the publisher** (only the authorized item is published).
    10. **item limit: non-advancing cursor fails loud** — a stuck adapter that re-serves the same key raises `RuntimeError: sync cursor did not advance` (no infinite loop, no silent skip).
    11. **item limit: `max_pages` fails loud** — an endless advancing listing is cut off by `max_pages=3` (`exceeded max_pages`), bounding the run.
    12. **byte limit — honest disclosure** — a 200 KiB item is handed straight to the publisher (created, not rejected), pinning that the **sync path does not gate item bytes**; byte/time budgets belong to the ingestion layer (T-V1-031 `packages/ingestion/budget.py`), disclosed rather than freelanced here.
WHY: spec goal "prove exactly-once convergence with fixtures, including failures" + instructions 1–2 + reviewer checklist. The suite locks the three T-034 metrics at the convergence level: **convergence 5/5** (create/update/delete/reorder/retry), **duplicates 0** (`duplicate_versions()` empty everywhere + per-id `calls` has no repeated checksum), **silent skips 0** (keyset resume + the two fail-loud item-limit guards). It also proves the "no duplicate published versions" property the T-034 handoff explicitly deferred to this ticket (RISKS c: "wiring real publication and proving no duplicate published versions is T-V1-035") — at this boundary, via the `publisher` seam and per-item outcome recording. No DECISIONS row needed (no fork/build choice; pure test-append on the accepted T-034 architecture).
REUSED: **no new dependency** (`pyproject.toml`/`package.json` untouched). Reuses the **production** code paths, not re-implementations: `connectors.interfaces.page_items`/`snapshot_digest` (keyset paging + snapshot digest), `connectors.sync_consumer.{QueuedSyncConsumer, InMemorySyncProgressStore, SourceIdentity, ItemStatus, encode_sync_job}`, the **real** `LocalFolderConnector` (unauthorized-path test), and `core.errors.{ReasonCode, canonical_code, normalize_code}` (T-V1-025 taxonomy). All fakes (`Source`, `SourceConnector`, `PublishedStore`, `LeaseQueue`, `InterruptingPublisher`, `FlakyConnector`) are test-local.
BUILT: nothing custom in product — the harness fakes (source/publisher/queue) are the smallest fixtures that exercise the real consumer's seams; a real durable publication store + real Redis lease were already proven by T-034 (its restart + queue round-trips), so this suite reuses the same in-memory seams for deterministic, service-free convergence proofs.
VERIFY (repo `Super Rag/knowledge-os`, venv `/root/ko-venv`, offline):
  - Ticket gate → `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_sync_convergence.py -q` → **`12 passed in 0.39s`**.
  - Connector/sync surface (no pre-existing test touched or weakened) → `pytest tests/unit/test_sync_convergence.py tests/unit/test_sync_consumer.py tests/unit/test_connector_source_contracts.py tests/unit/test_connectors.py tests/unit/test_git_connector.py tests/unit/test_web_connector.py tests/contracts -q` → **`212 passed, 1 warning`**.
  - Full unit gate → `pytest tests/unit -q` → **`970 passed, 1 failed`**; the single failure `tests/unit/test_format_matrix.py::test_cell_pdf_digital_reads_the_whole_document_with_pages` is **foreign/parallel-agent (T-V1-031) and proven independent of my file**: that file is **untracked** (`?? tests/unit/test_format_matrix.py`, absent from HEAD), imports **nothing** from `connectors`/`sync_consumer` (`grep -cE "connectors|sync_consumer"` → `0`), and with **my new file moved out of the tree** (`mv` → re-run → `mv` back) it **still fails** (`1 failed`), after which my file was restored and re-verified green (`12 passed`).
  - Lint/type → `ruff check tests/unit/test_sync_convergence.py` → **`All checks passed!`**; `mypy tests/unit/test_sync_convergence.py` → **`Success: no issues found in 1 source file`**.

METRICS: **convergence 5/5 scenarios** (create, update, delete, reorder, retry — tests 1–5); **duplicates 0** (`store.duplicate_versions() == {}` asserted across every scenario + interrupt/reorder paths; per-id publish calls carry all-distinct checksums); **silent skips 0** (keyset resume covered by the reorder+interrupt test and the delete test; the two item-limit guards fail loud instead of looping). Plus failed-visible-retryable (canonical code + recovery), unauthorized-excluded (real-adapter symlink escape, visible + unpublished), and the byte/time/item limit verifications/disclosures in tests 9–12.
REVIEWER CHECKLIST: **(1) reorder covered** — the dedicated reorder scenario (order perturbation → identical head, zero extra publishes) **and** reorder composed with interrupt-resume (keyset cursor `k:b` still serves exactly `c,d` after a shuffle). **(2) interrupt-resume covered** — three angles: kill-mid-sync via **lease redelivery** (retry scenario), direct **mid-page crash** proving the re-served already-outcomed sibling is **not** re-published, and reorder+interrupt; each asserts resumed `True`, correct set, `duplicate_versions()=={}`. **(3) failures visible** — the failed-item test pins the canonical `ingestion_error` code (round-trips `canonical_code`) + recovery, and the unauthorized-path test pins a visible `failed` outcome for the excluded item.
RISKS: low (test-only, zero product-file changes; full unit suite + ruff + mypy at baseline).
  (a) **DISCLOSED — byte limit not in the sync path:** `size_bytes` is recorded on outcomes but never gated; a large item is published as-is (test 12 pins this). Byte/time budgets live in ingestion (T-V1-031 `packages/ingestion/budget.py`), NOT here — flagged so nobody reads the consumer as size-limiting. Did **not** freelance a byte cap (outside the allowlist).
  (b) **DISCLOSED — no end-to-end run wall-clock deadline:** `run_sync` has no total-time cap; the run is bounded by the item guards (`max_pages`, non-advancing-cursor) plus **transport** timeouts (web `httpx timeout=30.0`, git `subprocess timeout=60`) and the queue **lease** `timeout_seconds`. A single hung `fetch` that respects its transport timeout but never returns within a global budget is bounded only by that transport timeout, not by the consumer. A run-level deadline would be a small product follow-up — not freelanced here.
  (c) **DISCLOSED — delete is "no resurrect", not tombstone:** this seam guarantees a deleted item is never re-published and drops out of the live set; actually deleting the already-published document's vectors/rows is ingestion's T-V1-017 (out of scope, out of allowlist).
  (d) **DISCLOSED — exactly-once is at the version level, not invocation level:** the consumer publishes then records the outcome per item; a crash in the narrow window *between* a successful publish and its outcome record would re-publish on redelivery. That is safe because publication is idempotent (same checksum → same version, `duplicate_versions()` stays empty) — the same at-least-once posture T-034 documented. The interrupt fixtures raise *before* a successful publish (the common case), where invocation is exactly-once; the idempotent-version property covers the residual window.
  (e) **Foreign churn during the ticket, NOT mine** (never opened/edited/staged/reverted): HEAD stayed `c86cbfb` (no parallel commit landed on my paths); `M apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py`, `M packages/answer/engine.py`, `M packages/ingestion/adapters/*.py`, `M handoffs/{CODE1,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,COORDINATOR,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md`, `?? packages/ingestion/{format_matrix,budget}.py`, `?? packages/ingestion/adapters/pdf_scan.py`, `?? tests/fixtures/formats/`, `?? tests/unit/test_format_matrix.py`, `?? docs/ingestion/`, `?? handoffs/*.md` — all pre-existing or parallel T-V1-031/format-matrix work. The 1 remaining unit failure is inside that foreign set and was reproduced with my file absent (VERIFY).
  (f) **No commit / no deploy / no service restart / no migration / nothing staged by me.**

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-046 (deterministic validation failures park, never retry) — code2 (solo, allowlisted)

TASK: T-V1-046. Repo `Super Rag/knowledge-os`, spec `../rag-fork-solution/tasks/MICRO-TICKETS-V1.md` §V2 appendix. Start HEAD `01e1add` (the ticket pin) → end HEAD `62a55c8` — moved by **ONE foreign commit**, dev1's T-039 batch, whose diff is **empty on every one of my paths** (`git diff --stat 01e1add..62a55c8 -- apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py` → no output). Allowed files: *worker job error mapping + boundary error codes + tests*.

**The defect, traced to its origin.** T-V1-044 removed the pipeline's per-caller zero-vector guard so the T-V1-015 store boundary became the single enforcement point, and disclosed the one behaviour delta it caused (`handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md`, T-V1-044 row, RISKS (a)): *"A zero vector reaching `qdrant.upsert` now raises `VectorStoreInvalidVector(REJECT_ZERO)`, which the pipeline's `except Exception` wraps as **`qdrant_upsert_failed` (retryable=True)** instead of the old `ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED` (retryable=False) … the job burns its retry budget first … If museR wants 'rejects are non-retryable', the mapping belongs in the **shared** error handling (or the boundary raising a typed error the worker maps once), **not in a per-caller guard**."* museR ruled that way and cut this ticket. That is exactly the seam implemented — no per-caller guard was reintroduced anywhere.

CHANGE (3 paths + this row — all inside the allowlist; **zero product files beyond the mapping and the boundary contract note**):
- `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py` — **the single shared mapping.** New `classify_pipeline_failure(exc) -> IngestPipelineError` + a cycle-safe `_exception_chain` helper (walks `__cause__` **and** `__context__`). `record_failure` now takes its verdict from it **once, at the top**, with one line (`exc = classify_pipeline_failure(exc)`), so the code persisted (`normalize_code(exc.code)`, substring preserved for the T-V1-025 contract pin) and the retry/park decision taken below it can never disagree. A `VectorStoreInvalidVector` anywhere in the chain ⇒ re-issued as `IngestPipelineError("ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED", "storage boundary rejected the embedding (<reason>): <msg>", retryable=False)`; **everything else is returned unchanged** (`is exc` — the identical object, not a copy), so transient verdicts are untouched. The adapter import is deliberately **lazy inside the function**, matching `ingest_pipeline` and `ingestion.dedup`, so `import jobs.ingest_worker` still does not pull the Qdrant SDK. Module docstring + a header block record the rule and why it lives here.
- `packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py` — **docstring only (9 lines, zero code).** `VectorStoreInvalidVector`'s contract now states that every `REJECT_*` reason is deterministic, is mapped *exactly once* by the worker onto the non-retryable `ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED` park, and that transient failures stay plain `VectorStoreError` with their retryable verdict. This is the boundary side of the same contract, so the mapping is discoverable from where the error is raised.
- `tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py` — **NEW, 16 tests**, service-free (no Redis/Postgres/Qdrant; in-file fakes + a stub Qdrant client). (1) *Deterministic parks* — all 3 reasons (`zero_vector`, `non_finite`, `dimension_mismatch`) arriving wrapped exactly as the pipeline wraps them park on the **first** attempt: `status == "failed"`, `error_code == "ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED"`, `schedule_retry` never called, `finished_at` set, precise reason still in `error_message`; plus classifier-level pins, a deeper-chain wrap, and a cause-cycle safety test. (2) *Transient stays retryable* — a `VectorStoreError`-caused failure keeps `status == "queued"`, `error_code == "QDRANT_INDEXING_FAILED"` and its **unchanged `2**attempts(0) = 1s` backoff**; non-boundary failures return `is exc`; retry-exhaustion parking is re-pinned as intact. (3) *Zero writes either way* — a rejected vector performs **zero** client upsert calls and leaves the seeded index untouched, with a valid control batch that **does** land (so the zero-write assertion is not vacuous), and an end-to-end park-path test asserting nothing written *and* nothing retried.
- `handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md` — this row.

WHY: spec goal *"Invalid vectors fail fast-and-final instead of burning retries"* + the T-V1-044 ruling above. The three `REJECT_*` reasons are properties of the **vector**, not the service: a retry replays the identical input, so every retry is doomed by construction — the registry already says so (`ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED` = INDEXING / 422 / **retryable=False**, message "An invalid embedding vector was rejected at the storage boundary"). The bug was purely that the pipeline's generic `except Exception` on the upsert leg lost that information on the way to the worker. Fixing it in the worker's shared error handling — not in the pipeline — is what keeps T-V1-044's *"single boundary, not per-caller checks"* literally true, and it needs **no registry change at all**.

REUSED: **none** — no dependency, no new product file, no registry edit, no migration, no schema. It reuses the already-landed typed boundary error (`VectorStoreInvalidVector` + its stable `REJECT_*` reasons, T-V1-015) and the already-registered `ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED` spec (T-V1-025); the test harness follows the fake-session/`schedule_retry` seam `tests/unit/test_queue_semantics.py` already uses and the stub-client pattern `tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py` already uses.

BUILT: one 12-line pure classifier + a 9-line cycle-safe chain walker, plus the tests. Nothing else custom — no second zero-vector implementation, no per-caller guard, no parallel taxonomy.

VERIFY (venv `/root/ko-venv`, repo root, no deploy/restart):
- **Ticket gate** → `pytest -q tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py` → **16 passed**.
- **Both-paths + boundary + pipeline + queue + taxonomy** → `pytest -q tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py tests/unit/test_ingest_pipeline.py tests/unit/test_queue_semantics.py tests/contracts/test_error_taxonomy_contract.py` → **73 passed**; plus `pytest -q tests/unit/test_leg_failure_statuses.py` → **32 passed, 1 warning**.
- **The pin bites pre-fix (not a tautology).** With the single classification line temporarily disabled in my own file (saved/restored from `/tmp/opencode/tv1046/`), the same suite → **7 failed, 9 passed**: every deterministic-park assertion fails (`failed`→`queued`, `ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED`→`QDRANT_INDEXING_FAILED`, `scheduled == []`→one retry queued) while the transient, classifier-identity and boundary-zero-write tests pass — i.e. exactly the doomed-retry defect, and exactly the two behaviours the ticket says were already right. File restored byte-identical (`diff -q` clean) and re-verified **16 passed**.
- **Full gate** → `pytest -q -m "unit or contract" -p no:randomly` → **825 passed, 3 failed, 418 deselected**, and all 3 are the pre-existing baseline already documented at T-V1-049/T-V1-053, each re-proven independent of my diff: (i) `test_acceptance_probes.py::test_acceptance_5_multilingual_cross_language_retrieval` fails standalone with an **abstention** ("I cannot answer this question…") because it needs live embeddings/retrieval; (ii)+(iii) `test_ingest_pipeline.py::test_queue_keys_are_namespaced_per_env` and `test_queue_semantics.py::test_claim_keys_are_namespaced_like_the_rest` **both pass in isolation** (`2 passed`) and fail only in a full session because `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py:61-62` assigns `os.environ["INGEST_QUEUE_PREFIX"] = f"t021-…"` at import time and leaks — a pre-existing isolation bug unrelated to retry semantics. None of the 3 touches my files; my 5 targeted files are green in both runs.
- **Lint/type** → `ruff check apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py` → **All checks passed!**; `mypy apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py` → **Success: no issues found in 2 source files**.
- **Live-shape check after the T-039 commit landed** → re-read `ingest_pipeline.py`'s upsert leg: still `except Exception as exc: … raise IngestPipelineError("QDRANT_INDEXING_FAILED", f"Vector store upsert failed: {exc}", retryable=True) from exc` — the exact shape the mapping fires on, unchanged by T-039 (its 82 pipeline lines are in the parse/storage-write region).

METRICS: **doomed retries: 0** (a boundary rejection now schedules no retry at all — previously it burned `max_attempts` before parking); **transient retries preserved: yes** (unmoved code, unmoved backoff, non-boundary failures returned as the same object); deterministic reasons mapped: **3/3** to one canonical non-retryable code; per-caller guards reintroduced: **0**; registry/taxonomy changes: **0**; boundary write calls for an invalid vector: **0**; new tests: **16**.

REVIEWER CHECKLIST: **(1) deterministic parks** — the 3-reason parametrised test plus the outer-wrap and cycle tests assert terminal `failed` + `ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED` + `finished_at` + zero scheduled retries, and the pre-fix control shows they bite. **(2) transient retries** — the transient test asserts `queued`, the untouched `QDRANT_INDEXING_FAILED` code, the untouched 1s backoff and `finished_at is None`, and `classify_pipeline_failure` returns non-boundary failures as the identical object. **(3) zero writes either way** — a rejected vector drives the **real** `QdrantVectorStore.upsert` to zero client calls with the seeded index untouched, a valid control batch proves the stub is genuinely reachable, and the park path schedules no retry, so the bad vector is neither written now nor re-attempted on redelivery.

RISKS / DISCLOSED:
- **(a) One code covers three reasons — deliberate, disclosed.** `dimension_mismatch` and `non_finite` are now also reported as `ZERO_VECTOR_REJECTED`, because that registry entry's *documented* semantics is already generic ("An invalid embedding vector was rejected at the storage boundary", INDEXING/422/non-retryable) and it is the code the pre-T-V1-044 system used. A second code for the same condition would risk the "no parallel taxonomy" lock for no behavioural gain. The precise `REJECT_*` reason is therefore preserved verbatim in `error_message` so no operator detail is flattened away. If museR prefers distinct wire codes per reason, that is a 3-line registry addition + a one-line change in the classifier — flagging rather than freelancing it.
- **(b) I did NOT touch `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` — the warned T-039 live file.** The obvious fix site (catch `VectorStoreInvalidVector` on the upsert leg) is inside that live file, and T-V1-044's ruling explicitly ruled it out as a per-caller guard anyway. The mapping therefore lives in the worker's shared error handling, which is (i) the placement the T-V1-044 disclosure prescribed, (ii) a file that was **clean in git at both my start and end snapshots**, and (iii) structurally immune to the T-039 merge. Whoever merges must take my 3-file diff **and** the already-committed T-039 batch; they do not overlap (`git diff 01e1add..62a55c8` on my paths is empty).
- **(c) The mapping is only as wide as the cause chain.** It fires on the typed `VectorStoreInvalidVector` wherever it sits in the chain, which covers the pipeline's `raise … from exc`. A future leg that *swallows* the boundary error and raises a fresh one without chaining it would bypass the park (and fall back to today's retryable behaviour — safe, not corrupt: zero writes still hold at the boundary). The docstring on the boundary class now states the contract, so a new caller can see it.
- **(d) FOREIGN CHURN DURING THE TICKET — not mine; never opened, staged, edited or reverted.** HEAD moved `01e1add → 62a55c8` (dev1's T-039 commit, 14 paths) plus 4 foreign paths appeared/changed in the working tree afterwards. See the self-report below.
