## 2026-10-13 T-V1-054 (crash fixture reaps its workers — zero orphans) — code2 (solo, allowlisted, BOARD PRIORITY)

TASK: T-V1-054. Repo `Super Rag/knowledge-os`. Start HEAD `c907b66` → end HEAD `c907b66` (**UNMOVED**). Allowed files: `tests/fixtures/scripts/t021_crash_worker.py` + `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` **teardown/fixture code only**.

**Why this was an incident, not a test smell.** A stall in this fixture is *unbounded by design* (`await asyncio.Event().wait()` never returns), and the only thing that ever stopped a stalled worker was the parent's teardown. So a parent that was SIGKILLed, detached from its terminal, or lost mid-run left the worker wedged forever — 29 of them at ~140 MB RSS is the ~4 GB OOM that starved this box. Two independent causes had to be closed, because each covers the other's blind spot.

CHANGE (2 paths):
- `tests/fixtures/scripts/t021_crash_worker.py` — **self-termination watchdog (T-V1-054)**. `_parent_is_alive()` tests the parent's existence with signal 0 (sends nothing; `PermissionError` ⇒ alive, `ProcessLookupError` ⇒ gone). `_install_parent_watchdog()` starts a daemon thread that polls every `_WATCHDOG_INTERVAL = 1.0s` and `os._exit(0)`s the moment the parent is gone — deliberately a hard exit, because a stalled `Event().wait()` can never unwind and nothing should do partial work on the way out. `main()` reads `T021_PARENT_PID` and arms it **before the heavy imports**, so a parent that dies during start-up cannot leave us behind.
- `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` — **deterministic group reaping.** (a) `subprocess.Popen(..., start_new_session=True)`: each worker leads its own session/process group, which is what makes a `killpg` safe — without it the worker shares pytest's group and the teardown would kill the test runner, and a dead parent would leave the children behind. (b) `T021_PARENT_PID` passed to the child. (c) Module-level `_SPAWNED` registry; a worker is registered in a **`finally`** so one that spawned but failed to come up is still ours. (d) `_reap_all_workers()` kills each group with `os.killpg(os.getpgid(pid), SIGKILL)` — **group kill, not a best-effort `terminate`**, so anything the worker started dies with it — then waits a bounded `_REAP_GRACE_SECONDS = 5.0`. (e) `kill()`/`stop()` now signal through `_killpg()` with a bare-PID fallback; `kill()` still delivers a real SIGKILL, so the scenario's "no finally, no ack, no lease release" semantics are unchanged. (f) A **session-scoped `autouse` fixture** `_no_orphan_workers` records a baseline, reaps at teardown, and then **asserts** that no `t021_crash_worker` outside that baseline survived — a real `pgrep -f` (pattern bracketed so it cannot match its own argv). (g) `atexit.register(_reap_all_workers)` as belt-and-suspenders.
- `handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md` — this row.

**Not touched, deliberately:** no test bodies, no assertions, no timings, and T-V1-057's `_queue_namespace` fixture stays intact. Verified structurally — every hunk is an import, the new module-level block, or a `_CrashWorker` lifecycle method.

WHY: the card's checklist asks for deterministic reaping, and explicitly warns that *atexit in the test process is not enough if the process is SIGKILLed*. So neither net is trusted alone: the parent-side group kill handles the normal case, and the worker's own parent-watchdog handles the case the parent cannot. An orphan is now impossible by two independent routes rather than merely unlikely.

REUSED: none — no dependency, no fixture file, no conftest change. Both files are test-only.

BUILT: one watchdog (worker side), one registry + one reaper + one session fixture (parent side). No product code, no change to what the suite proves about crash recovery.

VERIFY (venv `/root/ko-venv`, live stack, **run ATTACHED in the foreground shell only**, crash-suite file only, no whole integration dir, no backgrounded pytest):
- **THE METRIC — orphans after suite: 0** → after `pytest tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py -q` (277.68s, exit 1 on test failures): `pgrep -af "t021_crash[_]worker"` → **no matches**; cross-checked with raw `ps -eo pid,ppid,rss,etimes,cmd | grep t021.crash` → **none**. (A raw `grep -c "crash_worker"` reports 2 — those are the two `grep` processes themselves; with `grep -v grep` the listing is empty. Reporting the number I can defend, not the flattering one.)
- **Watchdog proven in both directions** (throwaway probe outside the repo): a **dead** parent pid ⇒ `parent_gone … -> self-terminating`, process exits **0 in 0.75s**; an **alive** parent pid ⇒ `STILL_ALIVE_OK` after 6s, correctly not terminated.
- **Group-kill reaper proven in isolation**: a wedged process spawned exactly as the suite does (`start_new_session=True`, pgid == pid) then `_reap_all_workers()` ⇒ **`REAP_OK`, took 0.00s, `poll() == -9`** (SIGKILL). Deterministic, not a best-effort race.
- **No new failures** → this run: **7 failed, 1 passed**. Baseline at HEAD (measured in T-V1-057): **8 failed**. The 7 failures are an exact **subset** of that baseline; the one that passed here, `test_after_claim_crash_is_redelivered`, belongs to the known pre-existing attempt-count flake documented in T-V1-057 (`final_attempts == 3`, want `1`). **I am not claiming it as a fix** — treat it as flaky until someone re-measures it.
- **Lint** → `ruff check tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py tests/fixtures/scripts/t021_crash_worker.py` → **All checks passed!**; `py_compile` on both → OK.

METRICS: **orphan workers after suite: 0** (was 29 observed on this box); orphan proofs recorded by three independent means (pgrep -af, ps listing, awk exact-match); watchdog termination latency on parent death: **0.75s**; group-kill reap latency: **0.00s**; workers spawned into a dedicated session: **100%**; workers registered even when start-up fails: **100%** (`finally`); new failures introduced: **0**; test bodies / assertions / timings modified: **0**.

REVIEWER CHECKLIST: **(1) reaping deterministic** — `os.killpg` over a per-worker process group created with `start_new_session=True`, bounded wait, and a measured `poll() == -9`; plus the watchdog for the parent-SIGKILLed case that no parent-side mechanism can reach. **(2) no suite slowdown abuse** — no blanket sleeps anywhere; the only waits are the pre-existing `wait_stalled`/`_wait_terminal` bounded loops; the new waits are `proc.wait(timeout=…)` and a 1.0s watchdog poll; the suite's wall clock is unchanged in kind (277.68s vs 249–275s baseline). **(3) orphans asserted absent post-suite** — the session fixture asserts it, using a real `pgrep -f` rather than a mock, and I verified the number independently.

RISKS / DISCLOSED:
- **(a) The watchdog costs one `kill(pid, 0)` per second per live worker.** Free, but it means a worker cannot outlive its parent by more than ~1s — which is the point. A test that *intentionally* detaches a worker and expects it to survive would break; no such test exists here (the recovery worker is still spawned by this process).
- **(b) `start_new_session=True` changes the child's session, not just its group.** It is detached from any controlling terminal, so it no longer receives terminal signals. That is precisely why we now reap explicitly — but it means nothing may rely on a Ctrl-C reaching the child.
- **(c) The orphan assertion is a real assertion and can therefore *fail* the suite.** If a future test deliberately leaves a worker running (or a stray predates this run and is not in the baseline), teardown now fails with its pid rather than passing quietly. That is the intended trade; the baseline set is recorded so a pre-existing stranger does not false-positive.
- **(d) `pgrep` must exist on the box** for the assertion to be meaningful; it is coreutils/procps and present here. If it were missing, `pgrep` returns empty and the assertion would pass vacuously — a small honesty caveat, disclosed rather than worked around.
- **(e) FOREIGN CHURN / box state — not mine.** dev1's live reindex had the box at **7 GB total / ~1 GB available** throughout; I kept to the crash-suite file only and ran everything attached in the foreground. `M handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` was dirty at start and end; the V4 stream and `handoffs/*` were never opened, staged, edited or reverted.

GIT SELF-REPORT (`git status --short` in `Super Rag/knowledge-os`; snapshots `/tmp/opencode/tv1054_{start,end}_status.txt`):
- **start** (HEAD `c907b66`, the ticket pin): 6 modified + 19 untracked, **all FOREIGN** — `M handoffs/{CODE1,COORDINATOR,DEV21-V4,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md`, `M packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`, `M tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py`, 16 `?? handoffs/*` + `?? tests/fixtures/workflows/` + `?? tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`. **None mine.** Index empty (0 staged).
- **end** (HEAD `c907b66`, **UNMOVED**): the same foreign set **plus exactly my 2 allowed paths** — `M tests/fixtures/scripts/t021_crash_worker.py` (+63) and `M tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` (+115/−6, lifecycle/fixture only).
- **start→end delta attributable to me = exactly these 2 paths plus this handoff row, both inside the allowed set. 0 foreign paths added, modified or removed by me. HEAD unmoved, 0 commits, 0 deploys, 0 restarts, 0 compose, nothing staged by me — index left empty.**

PROGRESS ROW — TASK: T-V1-054 crash fixture reaps its workers, zero orphans (implemented, evidence captured).
PROGRESS ROW — DONE: two independent nets make an orphan impossible — the worker now watches its parent and `os._exit`s within 1s of it dying (proved: 0.75s on a dead parent, correctly stays alive on a live one), and the parent spawns every worker into its own session and group-kills it at teardown with a real `pgrep` assertion that nothing survived (proved: reap 0.00s, `poll() == -9`). **`Orphan workers after suite: 0`**, verified three ways after a full attached run. 0 new failures (7 ⊂ the 8-test baseline; the one that passed is the known attempt-count flake, not claimed as a fix). No test bodies, assertions or timings touched; T-V1-057's fixture intact. worker_loop_starting crash_stage=parse
```
ingest_claims_recovered / ingest_claims_recovered / ingest_claims_recovered
```
— recovering claims repeatedly and never claiming a job to process.

BUILT: nothing. This ticket filed evidence and one follow-up, as instructed.

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-066 (crash harness: per-cycle teardown + marker-dir sweep) — code2 (solo, allowlisted)

TASK: T-V1-066. Repo `Super Rag/knowledge-os`. Start HEAD `405da23` → end HEAD `405da23` (**UNMOVED**). Allowed: `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` — **teardown/fixture/sweep code only**.

**T-V1-064's hypothesis is now pinned, with a mechanism.** Reading `_scenario`: when a stall stage never fires, `crash.wait_stalled()` raises **before** `crash.kill()` — there is no `finally` around the crash worker (only around `recovery`). So that worker survives into the next cycle, still holding the shared module-scoped `QUEUE_PREFIX`, and can claim the *next* cycle's job. That is the cross-cycle interference, and it explains why the leftover workers logged only `ingest_claims_recovered`: they were busy with someone else's job.

CHANGE (**1 path, +47/−1, 4 hunks — all fixture/harness**):
- `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` —
  **Per-cycle teardown.** `_await_workers_gone(timeout)` **polls** `proc.poll()` every `_CYCLE_POLL_INTERVAL = 0.1s` up to `_CYCLE_GONE_TIMEOUT = 10.0s` and returns the instant the last worker is actually gone — a bounded poll, not a blanket sleep (nothing sleeps for a fixed period hoping it was enough). `_CrashWorker.start()` calls `_reap_all_workers()` + `_await_workers_gone()` **before** spawning, so every cycle begins from a provably-empty worker set regardless of how the previous cycle exited (pass, assert-fail, or timeout). If a worker somehow survives the bound it prints a `WARNING t021: workers still alive…` with names rather than hanging. Placed in the harness class — **not** the scenario helper — deliberately: it needs no test-body or assertion edit and holds on *any* exit path.
  **Marker-dir sweep.** The module-scoped `marker_dir` fixture now removes the directory it created in a `finally`, so the FS no longer grows by one dir per run.
- `handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md` — this row.

WHY: two leaks (a process and a filesystem directory) that made the suite's failures look random. Both were in the harness, not the product, so both are fixed in the harness; per-cycle isolation is a *precondition* for the suite's results meaning anything.

REUSED: none — no dependency, no new file, no product code, no new fixture library. Reuses T-054's existing group-kill reaper; this ticket only decides *when* it runs.

BUILT: one bounded-poll helper, one pre-spawn handover in `start()`, one `finally` sweep. Nothing else custom.

VERIFY (venv `/root/ko-venv`, **only the two chosen tests, twice, attached in the foreground shell, never detached, never the whole 350s file**; dev1's reindex running, ~2 GB available):

- **THE METRIC — marker timing stable across 2 consecutive runs** (`test_crash_during_embed_is_redelivered` + `test_crash_at_publication_gate_is_idempotent`):

| run | embed | publication | wall clock | `t021-markers-*` dirs after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BEFORE** (T-064) | FAIL — `crash-4 never reached stage 'embed'` | FAIL — `crash-5 never reached stage 'publication'` | — | grew every run (28 accumulated) |
| **run 1** | **PASS** | **PASS** | 44.85s | **28 (unchanged)** |
| **run 2** | **PASS** | **PASS** | 86.75s | **28 (unchanged)** |

- **Markers really fired in both runs**, and this is not a proxy: `wait_stalled()` returns *only* when `marker_path.exists()`, so a passing test is proof the marker was written. Both tests pass in both runs ⇒ both stall markers fired 2/2 each.
- **Sweep proven, not assumed** → `ls -d /root/.tmp/t021-markers-*` counted **28 before run 1, 28 after run 1, 28 after run 2** — two full runs added zero directories.
- **Scope discipline** → `git diff -U0` = 4 hunks, all in `marker_dir`, the constants block, the reaper helpers and `_CrashWorker.start()`. `git diff | grep -E "^[+-].*def test_|^[+-].*assert "` → **no hits**: no test body and no assertion changed. `STAGE_TIMEOUT`/`TERMINAL_TIMEOUT` are **not in the diff at all** — the 45s constant is untouched, so I did not conclude the timeout was the bug.
- **Protected prior work verified present**: T-057 `_queue_namespace` (2 refs), T-054 nets (`start_new_session=True` / `os.killpg` / `_no_orphan_workers` / `T021_PARENT_PID`, 6 refs), T-065's `parse_with_precedence` seam (6 refs in the worker).
- **Lint/compile** → `ruff check` on the touched file → **All checks passed!**; `py_compile` → OK.
- **Hygiene** → **0 orphan** `t021_crash_worker` processes after both runs.

METRICS: marker firing stable across 2 consecutive runs: **yes (2/2 both stages, both runs)**; embed + publication **FAIL → PASS**; marker-dir accumulation per run: **1 → 0**; cross-cycle worker carry-over: **eliminated by construction**; `STAGE_TIMEOUT` changes: **0**; test bodies / assertions changed: **0**; product-code changes: **0**; files changed: **1**; hunks: **4**; orphans after runs: **0**.

REVIEWER CHECKLIST: per-cycle teardown is a bounded **poll** verified by `proc.poll()`, not a sleep, and it runs before every spawn so a leftover worker cannot claim the next cycle's job; the marker-dir sweep is scoped to the dir this module created and demonstrably adds zero directories across two runs; stability shown with a 2-run × 2-test table against the recorded BEFORE state.

RISKS / DISCLOSED:
- **(a) The sweep removes only *our own* marker dir, deliberately.** Older `t021-markers-*` dirs are left alone: they are diagnostic evidence — T-V1-064's entire root cause came from reading a failing worker's log out of one — so deleting another run's markers is not this harness's call. The **28 pre-existing accumulated dirs are still there**; the fix stops the bleeding, it does not clean the wound. A one-off `rm -rf /root/.tmp/t021-markers-*` is a coordinator call (needs dev1's reindex quiet).
- **(b) Worker logs are now swept, which removes post-hoc evidence.** That is the intended behaviour but it cuts both ways: after a future failure I can no longer read the worker's log unless the run is inspected mid-flight. If a future failure needs that log, the sweep should be made opt-in (e.g. `T021_KEEP_MARKERS=1`) rather than removed — flagged, not done.
- **(c) Per-cycle handover costs a reap + poll per spawn** (normally sub-millisecond, since the previous worker is already dead). Worst case it adds `_CYCLE_GONE_TIMEOUT` (10s) per cycle if a worker refuses to die; it does not sleep that long in the normal path.
- **(d) Two runs of two tests is a small sample.** The card asked for exactly this and that is what is shown — but I am not claiming the remaining flakiness is *eliminated*, only that both chosen stages passed twice consecutively with marker dirs stable. Wall clock varied 44.85s → 86.75s between identical runs, which on this RAM-pinned box is contention, not harness cost.
- **(e) This does not touch `wait_stalled`'s 45s `STAGE_TIMEOUT`.** If a stage genuinely needs longer than 45s on this box that would still fail — but the two stages I ran cleared it comfortably, so the constant is not currently the binding constraint and I left it alone per instruction.
- **(f) FOREIGN CHURN — not mine.** 25 paths dirty at start (V4 stream + `handoffs/*`); none opened, staged, edited or reverted. HEAD unmoved across my ticket.

GIT SELF-REPORT (`git status --short`; snapshots `/tmp/opencode/tv1066_{start,end}_status.txt`):
- **start** (HEAD `405da23`): 25 dirty paths, **all FOREIGN** — `M handoffs/{CODE1,COORDINATOR,DEV21-V4,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md`, `M packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`, `M tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py`, plus untracked `handoffs/*`, `tests/fixtures/workflows/`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`. **None mine.** Index empty.
- **end** (HEAD `405da23`, **UNMOVED**): the same foreign set **plus exactly my 1 allowed path** — `M tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` (+47/−1).
- 0 foreign paths added, modified or removed by me; 0 commits, 0 deploys, 0 restarts, 0 compose, index left empty, 0 orphans.

PROGRESS ROW — TASK: T-V1-066 crash harness per-cycle teardown + marker sweep (implemented, evidence captured).
PROGRESS ROW — DONE: T-064's hypothesis pinned with a mechanism — when a stall stage never fires, `crash.wait_stalled()` raises **before** `crash.kill()` (no `finally` around the crash worker), so it survives into the next cycle under the shared prefix and claims that cycle's job. Fixed in harness code only: `_CrashWorker.start()` now reaps and **polls** (0.1s ticks, 10s bound, not a sleep) until every prior worker is verifiably gone before spawning, and the module-scoped `marker_dir` fixture sweeps the dir it created. **METRIC: embed + publication both PASS in 2 consecutive runs (2/2 each), marker dirs 28 → 28 → 28 (zero accumulation across two runs), 0 orphans.** `STAGE_TIMEOUT` untouched (not in the diff), no test body/assertion changed, no product code. Risks: (a) pre-existing 28 dirs deliberately left (diagnostic evidence — the T-064 root cause came from one; the sweep is the coordinator's call), (b) sweep removes post-hoc worker logs — flagged, not undone, (c) sub-ms cost per spawn, (d) 2×2 sample does not prove all flakiness is gone and wall clock varied 44.85s→86.75s under contention, (e) 45s timeout deliberately not raised, (f) foreign churn disclosed.
PROGRESS ROW — NEXT: push DONE to w5:p4 (plain prompt, no --wait) and go idle.

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-067 (`T021_KEEP_MARKERS` opt-in) — code2 (solo, allowlisted)

TASK: T-V1-067. Repo `Super Rag/knowledge-os`. Start HEAD `5088180` (T-066 already committed, so my T-066 paths started clean) → end HEAD `5088180` (**UNMOVED**). Allowed: **the `marker_dir` fixture in `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` ONLY**.

CHANGE (**1 path, +36/−8, 2 hunks — both inside the fixture function**):
- `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` — `marker_dir` fixture only:
  - Fixture docstring now documents the escape hatch: name `T021_KEEP_MARKERS`, truthy spellings, **default OFF**, and *when* to use it (set → re-run the one failing test attached → read the logs from the printed path → unset), with the explicit warning that leaving it set re-accumulates a dir per run, which is the thing the fixture exists to prevent.
  - Teardown reads `os.environ.get("T021_KEEP_MARKERS", "").strip().lower() in {"1", "true", "yes"}`; when truthy it prints the surviving dir's path and skips the sweep, otherwise it runs T-066's `shutil.rmtree` exactly as before.
- `handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md` — this row.

WHY: T-066's sweep is right for accumulation but it also destroys the crash worker's `crash-N.log` / `recovery-N.log` — and those logs are *how T-V1-064's root cause was found*. So the correct fix made the next failure undiagnosable. The default must stay OFF (accumulation was the bug, and T-066 proved one dir per run is what accumulates); diagnosis needs an explicit, documented opt-in, not a policy change.

REUSED: T-066's `marker_dir` fixture body, its `shutil.rmtree` sweep, and its "our own dir only" scoping — unchanged. No new dependency, no new file, no product code, no new module-level symbol (the truthy check is inline in the fixture, deliberately, to stay inside the narrow allowlist).

BUILT: one inline env read, one `if/else`, one print. Nothing else.

VERIFY (venv `/root/ko-venv`; **no crash-file run at all for this ticket**, per the card — dev1's reindex holds the box):
- **(1) Behavioural proof — direct fixture invocation**, `/tmp/opencode/tv1067_proof.py` (outside the repo → no dirty path). The fixture is unwrapped from pytest's decorator and driven by hand (`next(gen)` → write a stand-in `crash-1.log` → `gen.close()` to run the `finally`), for 13 env values. Deterministic, no services, no 350s test:

| `T021_KEEP_MARKERS` | expected survive | actual |
|---|---|---|
| `<unset>` (default) | False (swept) | **False** |
| `''` / `'0'` / `'false'` / `'no'` | False | **False** |
| `'maybe'` (unrecognised) | False | **False** |
| `'1'` / `'true'` / `'yes'` | True | **True** |
| `'TRUE'` / `'YES'` / `'TrUe'` (case) | True | **True** |
| `'  yes  '` (whitespace) | True | **True** |

→ **PROOF: PASS — 13/13 cases behaved as documented.** Unrecognised values fall back to OFF, so a typo cannot silently start accumulating dirs.
- **Default preserved**: the `<unset>` row is the direct proof that T-066's sweep is still the default — this ticket did not weaken it.
- **Proof leaked nothing**: marker dirs **28 before, 28 after**, leaked-by-proof **0**. (First run of the proof reported FAIL — that was *my proof's* bug: it counted the 28 pre-existing T-064/066 dirs as its own leak instead of differencing against a baseline. Fixed to a baseline-relative check and re-run; the 13 behaviour rows were correct on both runs.)
- **`if/else`, not an early `return`** — and I verified the reason rather than asserting it: `gen.throw(ValueError(...))` into the fixture propagates correctly under **both** `KEEP` unset and `KEEP=1`. A `return` inside a generator's `finally` would have swallowed a teardown exception.
- **(2) ruff + py_compile** on the touched file → `All checks passed!` / `COMPILE OK`.
- **Scope discipline** → `git diff -U0` = **2 hunks, both inside `marker_dir`**. `git diff | grep -E "^[+-].*def test_|^[+-].*assert |STAGE_TIMEOUT|TERMINAL_TIMEOUT"` → **no hits**: 0 test bodies, 0 assertions, 0 timeout constants changed.
- **Prior work intact** → T-066's `_reap_all_workers` / `_await_workers_gone` still present (6 refs); T-057's `_queue_namespace` and T-054's reap nets untouched.

METRICS: env values proven: **13/13**; truthy spellings documented and tested: **3** (`1`/`true`/`yes`) + case + whitespace tolerance; default still OFF: **yes**; dirs leaked by verification: **0**; files changed: **1**; hunks: **2** (both in the fixture); test bodies / assertions / timeouts / product-code changes: **0**; commits: **0**.

RISKS / DISCLOSED:
- **(a) The opt-in is per-run, not sticky.** Nothing enforces unsetting it, and an exported `T021_KEEP_MARKERS=1` in a shell profile would silently re-accumulate a dir per run — reintroducing exactly the leak T-066 fixed. The print states the path each time so the dirs are at least visible. Not adding expiry/auto-clean, as that would be a second policy this ticket was not scoped for.
- **(b) The docstring is the only user-facing documentation.** Not added to `docs/` or a README — both are outside this ticket's allowlist. If the crash harness is documented elsewhere, this is the gap; flagging rather than freelancing.
- **(c) Truthiness is my spelling choice**, and `maybe`-style values deliberately read as OFF. Anyone expecting `T021_KEEP_MARKERS=on` to work will get a swept dir. Documented explicitly in the docstring; a future widening (e.g. accepting `on`) is a one-line change.
- **(d) No end-to-end crash-test run.** Per the card, verification is fixture-level and the crash file was deliberately not run (350s, contended box). The sweep/opt-in decision is the *only* thing this ticket changes and it is fully exercised; but I have not observed a real failing crash run's logs surviving a real pytest session. That is the one claim I am not making.
- **(e) FOREIGN CHURN — not mine.** 25 dirty paths at start (V4 stream + `handoffs/*`); none opened, staged, edited or reverted. T-066's own paths were clean at start because museR committed them (`5088180`).
